R 16CONCESSION LEDGER

CONCESSION LEDGER

16 · CONCESSION LEDGER

Every agent, every round, verbatim. The Adjudicator and Reader briefs carry no concession requirement, so those

five agents have no entry; the fourteen agents whose briefs require one all filed one.

Phase 1 — Cartographers (Round 0)

Cartographer, Territory 1 · retrieval calls: 37

CONCESSION: MOVED — I opened expecting a populated set of CAD-denominated 2026-maturity refinancings to map; the primary record instead shows the qualifying set is nearly empty, because the 2026-maturity retirements I could retrieve were funded by pre-window 2025 issuance (TELUS, Capital Power), by USD issuance (Nutrien), by revolver-directed proceeds (Waste Connections), by par-for-par exchange at identical terms (Enbridge), or paired with non-2026 tenders (Bell). Only Finning presents a clean in-window CAD pair, and its retired-versus-new coupon gap is close enough to the 150bp threshold that a single additional issuer would move the issuer-level median across it.

Cartographer, Territory 2 · retrieval calls: 47

CONCESSION: MOVED — I entered expecting the 2026 maturity-wall thesis to show a wide coupon step-up, and the retrieved territory does not carry one: the FTSE Canada 2026-maturity corporate index tracker prints a 3.2 percent weighted average retired coupon, its 2026-maturing non-financial names include Rogers at 5.650 percent and Suncor at 5.400 percent, in-window Canadian non-financial fixed-rate pricings I could date run 4.231 to 4.70 percent, and the largest single retirement I found (TELUS Series CZ) was funded by December 2025 hybrid notes that the population definition excludes. I could not retrieve an issuer-level roster of 2026 retirements matched to in-window replacements, so this ledger maps coupon levels on both sides rather than the median itself.

Cartographer, Territory 3 · retrieval calls: 39

CONCESSION: UNMOVED — The strongest objection to this ledger is that it never produces the issuer-level median itself: it maps the price level of 2026 CAD non-financial issuance (407 at 4.48%, Bell at 4.70%, CNR-vintage comparables near 3.75-4.55%) and scattered retired coupons (Rogers 5.650%, Suncor 5.400%, North West Redwater 2.000%, Capital Power 4.986%) without a matched issuer panel, so a critic can say the +150bp figure survives untested. It fails because the burden runs the other way. To clear 150bp the median Canadian non-financial issuer would need a retired 2026 coupon near 3.0% or below against a new coupon near 4.5%, and the retrieved 2026 cohort is not built that way: it is bimodal, containing legacy 5%-plus paper whose refinancing gap is negative, and the market it refinances into has the Bank of Canada at 2.25%, five-year GoC at 3.16%, and investment-grade spreads that the Bank of Canada itself placed in their lowest percentile on 28 May 2026 and RBC GAM called near 25-year tights on 20 March 2026. XCB's 4.25% yield to maturity sitting below its 4.37% weighted average coupon on 31 July 2026 is the cleanest single reading: at market levels, refinancing the average outstanding Canadian corporate bond lowers its coupon. Two further defects make the statistic unstable rather than merely small — Bell refinanced via tender offer with no dated 2026 target, and Enbridge took US$2 billion to the USD market in March 2026, both of which strip issuers out of a CAD-only, retire-then-replace panel; and pre-funders like Capital Power, which retired its January 2026 maturity in November 2025, never enter the 2026-settled window at all. I found no analyst or outlet on or before 2026-08-22 asserting a Canadian issuer-level median gap above 150bp, and none asserting one below it either; the named commentary I retrieved (Schleich/Currie/Lovely at National Bank, Cheah/Lee/Self at RBC GAM, BMO GAM, the Bank of Canada) argues the rate and spread backdrop, not the gap. That is the honest shape of this territory: thin on direct rebuttal, strong on the inputs that make the threshold hard to reach.

Cartographer, Territory 4 · retrieval calls: 28

CONCESSION: UNMOVED — The strongest objection is that the Registered Reports base rate comes from psychology, not fixed income, so importing 43.66 percent as the prior for a market-threshold claim is a domain transplant. It fails because the mechanism being priced is not psychological: it is the removal of post-hoc threshold selection. Both the psychology gap and the documented one-directional overshoot in professional twelve-month yield forecasts are instances of the same failure mode, and the two independent lines converge on a prior below one half.

Round 1

Inquisitor, Round 1 · retrieval calls: 40

CONCESSION: MOVED — I expected the issuer-level records to break under retrieval and most of them did not: coupons, sizes, maturities, closing dates and the fund and index prints came back matching the ledger to the digit, and two of the three market-level numbers I most doubted (the 79bp 5s30s spread and the lowest-percentile spread language) verified verbatim. What moved me is that the ledger's failures are not fabrication but construction — a misread AE coefficient at C-11, a log-assets-for-size swap at C-43, a size figure taken from the wrong Enbridge filing at C-19, and, above all, a headline built by selecting one tranche from one release while an Enbridge C$2bn CAD offering, a 407 2056 tranche, a Waste Connections 2036 tranche and Bell's own M-70 sat unrecorded. I remain unmoved that a 150 basis point figure survives: it is a one-decimal comparator, a financial-dominated 0.2-duration coupon set, and a tenor gap the ledger's own two July 2026 fund documents measure at 185bp.

Null, Round 1 · retrieval calls: 28

CONCESSION: MOVED — I expected the shared-source map to be the main lever and it was not. I opened each cited document instead of arguing from the correlation table, and the E0/E1 evidentiary spine held under direct checking, record by record, including every issuer pricing release and both fund profiles; a single authoritative source is sufficient for a claim about what that source states, and I concede that 41 of 48 records are settled. What retrieval gave me instead was sharper than what I came in with: Canadian Natural's 4 December 2025 prospectus prices 3-, 5- and 10-year paper for one issuer on one day at 3.30/3.75/4.55, so 125 of the disputed 150 basis points is reproduced by tenor alone with credit held constant — which makes the term-premium rival not merely available but measured. I also did not expect to find an E0 record falsified on its own falsifier, but C-11's range drops the "Other AEs" bucket at 7.1 pp, the one grouping that contains Canada. I remain unmoved on the load-bearing inferential spine: C-22 subtracts a financial-heavy index statistic from a transaction that refinanced 2028–2047 paper, calls the residual credit repricing in a ledger whose own records place spreads at their lowest percentile, and reports "exactly 150" from a one-decimal input — and the ledger contains a verified counterexample, Capital Power, refinancing a 2026 maturity 75 basis points cheaper.

Advocate, Round 1 · retrieval calls: 37

CONCESSION: MOVED — I came in expecting the round-number and reference-class inferences to be salvageable at reduced width and they are not, so I conceded all three of the run's unsourced or transplanted inferential records outright: C-09, because I ran the search its own falsifier specifies and no study of pre-registered numeric market or finance forecasts exists to transfer Scheel's 43.66% into finance; C-14, because retrieving RQO falsified its antecedent rather than merely leaving it unsupported — the largest 2026 holdings are short-dated low-coupon bank and caisse paper, which reverses the claim's sign; and C-17, because the round-number literature I retrieved establishes only that round numbers are selected, and the attention mechanism it documents points toward the Null's rival rather than away from it. On the headline I concede the mechanism and rebuild only the arithmetic: the defensible figure is roughly 130 bp across the four CAD 2036 prints, not "exactly 150" from one tranche of one book, and the retrieved decomposition runs against the record — a 3.56% ten-year Government of Canada yield leaves a ~95 bp new-issue spread against 93 bp market spreads sitting ~20 bp inside their long-term average, while the ten-year Government of Canada averaged 1.2-2.4 percent across the years the retired paper was sold. Credit moved the wrong way. I also found the falsifier mis-specified: it guards the harmless direction on the non-financial subset while the fatal direction is above 3.20 percent, where RQO's own non-financial lines sit near 4.4 percent. Where I did move against the attackers, I moved on retrieved discriminating evidence and not on rhetoric: the St. Louis Fed's -0.4 percentage-point mean error over 1993-2024 defeats the Null's regime-conditionality rival at C-10 because that sample contains the very rate surge the rival invokes and still carries the upward sign; and C-26 survives once detached from IFDP 1390, because the selection that operates in a tight-spread window is instrument, currency and tenor selection, which I verified five separate times in this run's own 2026 transactions — Bell, Waste Connections, TELUS, Nutrien and Enbridge each printed a coupon in a market or an instrument that was not where their 2026 maturity lived. C-04 also survives, re-warranted on the 185 bp gap between the ledger's own two July 2026 fund documents instead of on the 5s30s segment it wrongly cited. I remain unmoved that Sun Life's presence in RQO's top ten discredits that document: it is a fixed/floating debenture whose fixed term ends in 2026, and index inclusion on effective maturity is convention — though I could not confirm the call date, and I have marked that limitation rather than leaned on it.

Round 2

Inquisitor, Round 2 · retrieval calls: 35

CONCESSION: UNMOVED — the round-1 narrowings added detail, and the detail is where the records broke. Three of them (C-13, C-24, C-34) are now falsified by their own cited documents: the BMO outlook does publish index-level pricing work, Hirshleifer's ordering variable is within-day and not within-year, and Canso does name ICE BofA. Two load-bearing E2 constructions (C-04, C-26) rest on numbers and facts that appear in no source on their records — the 125 bp slope belongs to Canadian Natural Resources' December 2025 book, and IFDP 1390 is a stress-conditional paper conscripted to support a regime-independent claim it is designed not to make. The Capital Power trio is one press release counted three times.

Null, Round 2 · retrieval calls: 30

CONCESSION: MOVED — I entered expecting the correlation flags and the E2 inference chain to
carry most of the subset into "undetermined," and expecting the round-1 additions to be
uniformly unsupported. Neither held. Every E0 primary-document record I could fetch — the FEDS
Note's negative fact, the BoC sentence, the Scheel figures on both sides, Nutrien, Finning,
Bell's use of proceeds, Canso, BMO, Capital Power's pricing — verified at the source, several
verbatim, and in the strongest cases (C-36, C-34, C-02) the round-1 narrowing had already
retired the objection I would have raised. Eleven of nineteen are settled and I am not going to
pretend otherwise. What did not move me is the E2 tier: C-04's 125 bp slope is absent from both
its cited books, C-26 draws regime-independence from a paper measuring regime-dependence, and
C-22's pivotal 4.4% is a classification artifact that flips to 3.83% on the fund document's own
sector split. Two E0 records (C-24, C-43) survived on their arithmetic while misnaming what the
arithmetic measures — a within-day sequence called within-year, a stress-vs-normal contrast
called a comparison to the sample mean — and that is the failure mode this round should be
watching for, because a verified number attached to the wrong estimand reads exactly like a
verified claim.

Advocate, Round 2 · retrieval calls: 32

CONCESSION: MOVED — I entered intending to rebuild the round-1 text and rebuilt most of it, but three records could not be salvaged and two of those failed on evidence internal to them. C-26 is falsified by its own enumerated legs: TELUS printed C$800 million of Canadian-dollar tranches in the very transaction that redeemed its Canadian-dollar March 2026 maturity, and Bell both retired its C$750m March 2026 series and printed Canadian-dollar coupons twice in the window, so the record's central proposition has two counterexamples inside its own list before the regime-independence problem with IFDP 1390 is reached. C-36 is a true sentence with no evidentiary content, because the 2026 series it observes going unnamed had already been redeemed in December 2025. C-46 is a syndicated duplicate of C-20. Beyond the outright concessions I cut deeper than round 1 in four places, and in three of them on evidence I found rather than evidence the attackers supplied: C-22's comparator turns out to be 55.7% GOVERNMENT bonds at July 31, 2026, which invalidates the differential more decisively than the classification dispute either attacker pressed; C-43's "3.6, 3.5, 2.3 percent" is reconstructible as the coefficient divided by the sample-mean LOG assets, a percentage of a logarithm, replaced here with the correct ~46/41/24 percent level differentials; and C-29's round-1 addition is withdrawn because I searched for the 1993-2024 comparator and could not retrieve it at all. What did not move me is where the attacks over-reached: C-05's rival evidence agrees with the claim in direction and therefore does not discriminate; C-40's disputed sentence is vindicated by the Bank of Canada primary series against a non-persistent aggregator quote; C-22's median is drawn over 2036 prints and the "omitted" bonds mature 2031 and 2033; C-34's document names indices without attributing the 93 bp figure to any of them, so both attackers were half right; and C-45's replication confound, once corrected, leaves the comparison standing at 50.00 versus 95.95 percent. The pattern worth reporting is that the round-1 additions split cleanly: those that narrowed scope held, and those that added an unsourced supporting number or a characterisation of a document's form broke.

Round 3

Inquisitor, Round 3 · retrieval calls: 38

CONCESSION: MOVED — on C-02. I ran the note twice looking for surface and found none: the "within each rating category" methodology sentence is verbatim, the 2023:Q4-2026:Q3 projection window is verbatim ("We compute the historical series from 2000:Q1 until 2023:Q3 and project their evolution over the next three years, through 2026:Q3"), and the figure inventory is exhaustive exactly as the record enumerates it. The record narrowed itself to a checkable negative about one document and the document backs it; only the word "distributional" is loose. That is a record built the way records should be built, and my attack on it is the weakest line I filed.

Null, Round 3 · retrieval calls: 27

CONCESSION: MOVED — on two points I expected to win and did not. First, C-22: I came to argue the split was unresolvable because nobody had read the profile, and the profile turned out to print the table the record said no record reads — Government Bonds 55.7 / Corporate Bonds 44.3, AAA 0.0, Cash 0.0 — which closes Position A's escape route on Position A's own terms. The record understated what was available to it, and I had to re-found my contest on a different separator than the one it named. Second, the 4.231% coupon common to C-35 and C-46/C-48: I took that for a propagated figure, the cleanest correlation catch in the subset, and it verified at two unrelated primary documents a year apart. It is a coincidence. I record both against myself. I am unmoved on the eight contests above, and in particular on the two records — C-28 and C-35 — where the ledger reads a permissive use-of-proceeds enumeration as an allocation while correctly refusing to do so on C-48.

Advocate, Round 3 · retrieval calls: 39

CONCESSION: MOVED — on C-36 outright, and on the distinctive quantitative contribution of five further records. C-36 is the one I went looking to save and could not: Bell's Series M-43, C$650 million 2.90% due August 12, 2026, was outstanding and eleven weeks from maturity on the day of the release, it is absent from the ten series in the companion tender offer, and no redemption notice for it exists — so the record's "no such series existed" is false rather than merely uncited, and what remains is a documentary negative with no explanatory content. On C-04 I withdrew the 73 basis point floor, which was the record's only quantitative contribution: the Null is right that a contemporaneous curve slope cannot bound the non-credit part of a gap one side of which was priced years earlier, and re-sourcing the curve to the Bank of Canada showed the quoted values do not even reproduce for the stated date. On C-28 and C-35 I withdrew both allocation inferences, because the run is right that it applies the opposite and correct standard to the same species of sentence on C-48. On C-30 I withdrew the frame-inflation mechanism, because the frame-free samples that exist return the same 93-99 percent band. On C-48 I withdrew "no second source exists," having found the second source. Against that, three attacks failed on retrieval and I held or strengthened: C-34's spread figure genuinely carries no index attribution and the chart caption names no index; C-46's cited page carries an explicit no-review syndication disclaimer, which is exactly what the record says it is; and C-43's omitted Table 6 columns contain a United States GFC coefficient significant at 1 percent that cuts for the record, while C-22's contested Enbridge leg verifies as Canadian-dollar in Enbridge's own MD&A. Where I could not discriminate — the RQO "Government Bonds 55.7" classification — I narrowed the record to say the comparator cannot be certified in either direction, rather than pick the side that helps it.

CONTROL ARM

17 · CONTROL ARM

CONTROL_ARM: YES. One agent, one context, no subagents, same question, same cutoff, same tools.

Budget. Granted 457 retrieval calls — the Crucible run's counted total across all four Cartographers,

three Inquisitors, three Nulls and three Advocates — plus MAX_ROUNDS = 3 attack-and-revise passes. The tripwire

did not fire, so no tripwire retrieval is included on either side and the two budgets are matched on the same

basis. The control arm spent 204 of its 457 calls and completed all three passes.

Input-class note, stated plainly as §11 requires. The control arm's Reader was fed a draft conclusion,

not a verdict map. The instrument is identical — same brief, same four-line output form, same prohibition on

retrieval and on adding evidence — but the input class is not: one Reader read 48 adjudicated verdicts with

their bases, the other read one analyst's finished argument. That asymmetry is inherent to the comparison

rather than an artefact of how this run was set up, and it is a known limit on measurement 1 in §15.3: a

conclusion is a more forecast-shaped object than a map, so the control arm's Reader has an easier task and its

probability should not be read as evidence of better epistemics.

17.1 · Control arm full output — 07-control/control-arm.md, verbatim

Control arm — single analyst, one context

Question under test. Among Canadian non-financial investment-grade issuers that retired fixed-rate debt maturing in 2026 and replaced it with new fixed-rate issuance settled between 2026-01-01 and 2026-08-22, the issuer-level median new coupon exceeds the retired coupon by more than 150 basis points.

Cutoff: 2026-08-22. All sources cited are dated on or before that date.

RESEARCH NOTES

A. Rate environment — the mechanical driver of the claim

  • Bank of Canada, benchmark bond yields page, as of 2026-08-20 (primary): GoC 2yr 3.02%, 5yr 3.35%, 7yr 3.48%, 10yr 3.75%, long 4.15%. (bankofcanada.ca/rates/interest-rates/canadian-bonds/)
  • **Canso Investment Counsel, April 2026 Corporate Bond Newsletter, published 2026-04-24: the 5-year Canada yield rose from 0.54% (2021) to 3.10% (2026)** — a ~256 bp move in the risk-free rate for 5-year money. This is the strongest single fact behind the 2026 maturity-wall thesis.
  • **J. Zechner Associates, Bond Commentary — January 2026, published 2026-02-04: January 2026 saw $16.5bn of new Canadian corporate issues, a record January*; IG corporate spreads tightened 7 bp in the month to levels described as "multi-decade tight"* and comparable to 2004–2006.
  • **J. Zechner Associates, Bond Commentary — April 2026, published 2026-05-05: $22.1bn issued in April, third-highest month ever; IG spreads narrowed a further 8 bp; GoC 2yr and 5yr each +14 bp, 30yr +5 bp** on the month.
  • **Canso, January 2026 Corporate Bond Newsletter, published 2026-01-20 (updated 2026-01-29): 2025 Canadian supply was a record $160bn; long BBB spreads at 140 bp*, "72 bps inside the long-term average"*; TransCanada PipeLines priced a 30-year in mid-November 2025 at 145 bp / 5.127% coupon, its tightest long-bond spread since 1998.
  • Canso, April 2026: Canadian new issuance running ~30% ahead of 2025's pace year to date.
  • **Bank of Canada, Financial Stability Report 2026, published 2026-05-28*: "Investment-grade credit spreads compressed, falling to the lowest percentile in both Canada and the United States."*

The two forces cut against each other: risk-free rates up ~250 bp from the 2021 trough, but credit spreads at multi-decade tights, compressing the coupon step-up relative to the naive rate-differential story.

Critical structural observation: the 2026 maturity cohort is bimodal, not uniformly low-coupon. It contains 2016-vintage 10-years and 2021-vintage 5-years with coupons of 1.4–3.0%, but it also contains (i) legacy long bonds issued in the 1990s (TCPL 8.29%, Bell 8.875%) and (ii) short paper issued in 2023–24 at peak rates (Rogers 5.65%, Suncor 5.40%, Capital Power 4.986%). The maturity-wall thesis implicitly assumes the first group only.

Issuers meeting all the stated screens — non-financial, CAD, DBRS BBB- (BBB(low)) or better, retired a 2026-maturing fixed-rate CAD bond on or before 2026-08-22, and settled new CAD fixed-rate issuance between 2026-01-01 and 2026-08-22:

Median of the six: +110 bp. Drop Enbridge (legal-issuer mismatch): median of five = +92 bp. Treat Bell's 8.875% subordinated debenture as out of scope (Bell then = +188 bp): median of six = +135.5 bp; median of five (no Enbridge) = +143 bp.

Every configuration lands below +150 bp. The highest configuration I can construct without discarding evidence is +143 bp.

DRAFT CONCLUSION

Direction: the claim is not supported. On the evidence I can assemble, the issuer-level median coupon step-up is roughly +90 to +145 basis points, below the 150 bp threshold the claim asserts.

The claim's underlying mechanism is real and large. The 5-year Canada yield went from 0.54% in 2021 to 3.10% in 2026 (Canso, 2026-04-24), and GoC benchmarks at 2026-08-20 stood at 3.35% (5yr) and 3.75% (10yr) (Bank of Canada). An issuer rolling a 2021-vintage 5-year into a 2026-vintage 5-year does face something close to a 250 bp increase in the risk-free component. ARC Resources is close to the archetype: it redeemed C$450m of 2.354% Series 1 notes due March 2026 with C$950m of new 3.349%/4.104% paper on 2026-02-25, a +143 bp principal-weighted step-up. Finning is the cleanest single case in the whole set — a straight one-for-one refinancing of 2.626% August-2026 notes with 4.231% five-year notes settling two days before the maturity, +161 bp.

But three things push the median below the threshold.

First, credit spreads offset a large part of the rate move. Zechner (2026-02-04) called IG corporate spreads "multi-decade tight," Canso (2026-01-20) put long BBB at 140 bp — 72 bp inside its long-term average — and the Bank of Canada's Financial Stability Report (2026-05-28) recorded IG spreads in the lowest percentile in both Canada and the US. Bonds maturing in 2026 were largely priced when spreads were wider. The all-in coupon step-up is therefore materially smaller than the risk-free step-up.

Second, the 2026 maturity cohort is not uniformly low-coupon. It contains legacy long bonds (TransCanada PipeLines 8.29% due 2026-02-05, CAD 240.6m; Bell Canada 8.875% subordinated debentures due 2026-04-17, CAD 125m) and short paper issued at the 2023–24 rate peak (Rogers 5.65% Sept 2026, Suncor 5.40% Nov 2026, Capital Power 4.986% Jan 2026). For TransCanada PipeLines the principal-weighted retired coupon is 5.83% against new 10-year and 30-year CAD paper that on any plausible pricing comes in below that — a negative step-up. Capital Power, whose replacement settled just outside the window, refinanced a 4.986% January-2026 maturity at 4.231% — 75 bp cheaper.

Third, the issuers with the largest step-ups are systematically the ones that fall out of the panel. Hydro One retired 2.770% February-2026 paper and replaced it with US$1.0bn at 4.750% — a +198 bp step-up, but in US dollars, so outside a CAD-denominated screen. TELUS retired 3.75% and 2.75% 2026 paper but funded it from December 2025 hybrids, outside the settlement window. Their exclusion is required by the stated scope, not chosen.

The panel I can build has six issuers: TransCanada PipeLines (≈ −75 bp), Dream Industrial REIT (+18 bp), Bell Canada (+92 bp), Enbridge (≈ +128 bp), ARC Resources (+143 bp), Finning (+161 bp). Median +110 bp. The most claim-favourable defensible variant — dropping Enbridge for legal-issuer mismatch and treating Bell's subordinated debenture as out of scope — gives +143 bp. Still short of 150 bp.

What I am relying on. (i) The RQO/FTSE Canada 2026 Maturity index portfolio as at 2025-09-30 as my enumeration of the retired-side population, supplemented by issuer press releases for names it omits. (ii) Issuer primary disclosure — press releases, prospectus supplements, and quarterly financial statement debt notes — for every coupon in the panel except TransCanada PipeLines' June 2026 CAD tranches, whose coupons I could not retrieve and whose delta I have signed but not measured. (iii) Principal-weighting within issuer, one observation per issuer, as specified.

Where this is fragile. Six observations is a thin median; one added issuer moves it. The Enbridge entity question and the Bell subordinated-debenture question each move the median by ~15–35 bp. And the "replacement" matching is not identified by the claim: issuers systematically issued longer than what they retired (Bell retired 2016-vintage 10-year paper and issued 10s and 30s; Enbridge retired 2016-vintage paper and issued 5s, 10s and 30s), so part of any measured step-up is term premium, not refinancing cost.

PASS 1 — SELF-ATTACK

PROVENANCE

1. My population frame is a proxy and is provably incomplete. The retired-side enumeration rests on a single document: the RBC Target 2026 Canadian Corporate Bond Index ETF, 2025 Annual Financial Statements, Schedule of Investment Portfolio as at 2025-09-30 (verified header: "SCHEDULE OF INVESTMENT PORTFOLIO (in $000s) / RBC TARGET 2026 CANADIAN CORPORATE BOND INDEX ETF / September 30, 2025"). Three problems. (a) It is an ETF's holdings, not an index — RBC's own July 2026 monthly profile states "The Fund holds fewer than 25 holdings," i.e. it samples. (b) It tracks the FTSE Canada 2026 Maturity Corporate Bond Index, which is neither of the two indices named in the scope (S&P Canada IG Corporate; FTSE Canada Universe corporate sub-index). (c) Incompleteness is demonstrated, not hypothetical: eight qualifying 2026 CAD maturities I found from issuer sources are absent from it — Finning 2.626%, Keyera Partnership 3.96%, Capital Power 4.986%, TELUS 3.75% Series CV, Bell M-41 3.55%, Bell 8.875% subordinated, TransCanada PipeLines 8.29%, Dream Industrial 3.968% Series E. A frame that misses eight names I happened to find by other means is certainly missing others I did not.

2. The TransCanada PipeLines row — my only negative observation, and the one doing the most work pulling the median down — is the worst-sourced row in the panel. Both retired bonds (8.29% CAD 240.6m maturing 2026-02-05; 4.35% CAD 400m due 2026-05-12, redeemed early) come only from cbonds, a commercial data vendor. I could not open TC Energy's Q2 2026 debt note (the quarterly-report PDF does not carry a long-term-debt-issued/retired table at the level the fetch returned) and I could not open the Morningstar DBRS release of 2026-06-24 (the page renders client-side; every fetch returned site metadata only). No primary TC Energy document in my record confirms either retirement.

3. One cell of the panel was never measured at all. TCPL's June 2026 CAD tranches — CAD 700m due 2036 (CA89353ZCT36) and CAD 600m due 2056 (CA89353ZCS52), issued 2026-06-25 — have no retrievable coupons. cbonds lists them with coupon "unspecified" behind its paywall. I estimated 4.8–5.5%. A six-observation median with one estimated cell is a fragile object.

4. Bell's 8.875% subordinated debenture is an identification, not a stated fact. The coupon (8.875%), size (CAD 125m) and maturity (2026-04-17) come from the 1996 prospectus hosted on BCE's bondholder page. That it survived to 2026 and was repaid comes from Bell's prospectus supplement dated 2026-05-27, whose as-adjusted capitalization at 2026-03-31 reduces debt-due-within-one-year by exactly $125 million "maturing April 17, 2026." The equation of that $125m with this instrument is my inference from an exact amount-and-date match. Strong, but inferred.

5. Several exclusions are absence of evidence, not evidence of absence. I ruled 407 International, Nova Scotia Power, CT REIT, RioCan, Waste Connections and Gibson Energy out of the panel because I found no 2026 maturity for them — but I never retrieved their maturity schedules. Each is a candidate missing row. RioCan's and NSPI's releases say only "repay existing indebtedness."

6. Wire-service independence. barchart, stocktitan, tipranks, Yahoo Finance, The Globe and Mail, Manila Times, BOE Report, Cantech and newswire.ca/prnewswire are conduits for the same issuer press release. Where I used them I was reading the issuer's own text; none of them is a second source.

7. The fetch layer returned a confidently wrong table at least once. Two fetches of BCE's bondholder-info page gave mutually inconsistent answers about Bell's 2026 maturities — one asserting M-41 was the sole 2026 maturity among 62 issues, from a schedule dated 2026-06-30 by which point M-41 had been redeemed six months earlier and M-43 was still outstanding. I did not use that page for any load-bearing number, but it is a warning about the rest.

INFERENCE

My draft asserts a direction and a range: "the claim is not supported… the issuer-level median coupon step-up is roughly +90 to +145 basis points." The evidence supports something weaker.

The weaker claim I can actually defend: On a six-issuer panel assembled from retrievable public sources — a panel that is provably not the full population — the median step-up falls below 150 bp under every internally consistent screening convention I applied, and an independent market-level analogue lands near +100 bp. The claim is therefore unsupported by the retrievable record, and the balance of evidence tilts against it. It is not shown to be false.

The reason the stronger version overreaches is that a configuration exists which clears 150 bp:

| Screen applied | Panel | Median |

|---|---|---|

| All six as drafted | −75, +18, +92, +128, +143, +161 | +110 |

| Drop TCPL (weakest sourcing) | +18, +92, +128, +143, +161 | +128 |

| Drop TCPL and Enbridge (legal-issuer mismatch) | +18, +92, +143, +161 | +117.5 |

| Drop Enbridge only | −75, +18, +92, +143, +161 | +92 |

| Bell excl. subordinated debenture (Bell = +188), all six | −75, +18, +128, +143, +161, +188 | +135.5 |

| Bell = +188, drop Enbridge | −75, +18, +143, +161, +188 | +143 |

| REITs treated as financials (drop Dream), drop Enbridge, Bell = +188, require use-of-proceeds link (drops TCPL) | +143, +161, +188 | +161 |

That last row is internally inconsistent — a use-of-proceeds requirement that removes TCPL also removes Bell (whose June 2026 CAD proceeds went to tender offers for ten series of 2028–2047 CAD debentures and six series of USD notes, per its 2026-05-27 releases — none maturing in 2026) and Enbridge (February 2026 MTNs, general corporate). But the sensitivity is real and my draft understated it by presenting a range as if it were a measurement.

A second construction also cuts against the drafted precision: the claim says "median new coupon exceeds the retired coupon," which could be read as median(new) − median(retired) rather than median(new − retired). Computed that way: median new = (4.150 + 4.231)/2 = 4.19%; median retired = (2.85 + 3.864)/2 = 3.36%; difference +83 bp. Also below threshold, but a different number again.

SELECTION

A competent opponent would reach for market-level index data rather than my hand-built panel. I went and got it — and it runs against the claim, hard:

  • iShares Core Canadian Corporate Bond Index ETF (XCB), tracking the FTSE Canada All Corporate Bond Index™, as at 2026-08-20: weighted average coupon 4.38%, weighted average yield to maturity 4.32%, weighted average maturity 7.98 years. Cross-checked against the same fund's fact sheet as at 2026-07-31: coupon 4.37%, YTM 4.25%. Market-wide, the coupon Canadian IG corporates currently pay slightly exceeds the yield at which they could refinance. There is no aggregate upward coupon reset in this market at the cutoff.
  • iShares Core Canadian Short Term Corporate Bond Index ETF (XSH), tracking the FTSE Canada Universe + Maple Short Term Corporate Bond Index™, as at 2026-08-19: weighted average coupon 4.09%, YTM 3.80%, weighted average maturity 3.19 years. Cross-checked against its fact sheet as at 2026-07-31: coupon 4.08%, YTM 3.79%. For near-dated CAD IG corporates the average coupon exceeds market yield by ~29 bp.

The honest counter to my own use of those two: they are dominated by 2023–25 vintage high-coupon paper and are therefore the wrong vintage comparator for a 2026-maturity cohort. So I built the like-for-like version instead. Par-weighted average coupon of the non-financial 2026 CAD maturity cohort, computed from the RQO 2025-09-30 schedule: 3.285%. Against the twelve CAD non-financial IG new-issue coupons I catalogued for Jan–Aug 2026 (Enbridge 4.131 wtd; ARC 3.786 wtd; RioCan 4.308; Bell 4.783 wtd; 407 4.770 wtd; NSPI 3.947; Dream 4.150; CT REIT 4.357; Keyera 4.360 wtd; Gibson 4.450; Waste Connections 4.400 wtd; Finning 4.231), simple average ≈ 4.31%. Gap ≈ +102 bp — arrived at without using my panel at all, and landing in the same place.

I also went looking for rows an opponent could add to raise the median, and cleared them:

  • BCI QuadReal Realty — 1.073% CAD 400m matured 2026-02-04 (cbonds: "Matured"); a replacement would have produced a ~+300 bp delta. Its most recent note offering was the C$350m 3.281% green bond of 2025-03-14 — outside the window. No 2026 issuance. Out.
  • Saputo — 1.415% matured 2026-06-19; would have been the largest delta in the set. No CAD MTN offering in its 2026 news record. Out.
  • Capital Power — retired 4.986% due 2026-01-23, but its replacement (C$600m 4.231% due 2033) settled 2025-11-14, outside the window; and the delta was −75 bp anyway.
  • Rogers — C$500m and US$500m senior notes due 2026 still listed as outstanding on its bondholder page; no senior notes repaid in Q1 2026; its only 2026 CAD issuance was C$1.25bn 6.250% fixed-to-fixed-rate subordinated notes due 2056 (2026-03-27), a reset hybrid, not plain fixed-rate. Out on both legs.
  • Hydro One — retired 2.770% on 2026-02-24 and replaced with US$1.0bn 4.750% due 2031 (priced 2026-05-20). On a currency-agnostic screen that is a +198 bp row and would raise the median; the stated CAD-denominated scope excludes it. This is the single largest way the answer could move, and it is excluded by the question's own wording, not by my choice.
  • First Capital REIT (Series E, 2025-06-11), Granite, Primaris, SmartCentres, Choice Properties — no in-window 2026 CAD debenture issue found tied to a 2026 maturity.
  • Fortis — 2026 issuance was entirely USD at ITC and Central Hudson (Fortis Q1 2026 MD&A). Pembina, AltaGas — no 2026 debt offerings in their news records.

ANACHRONISM

Nothing. Every source in the record is dated on or before 2026-08-22 (latest: Bank of Canada yields 2026-08-20; XCB 2026-08-20; XSH 2026-08-19; Finning pricing 2026-08-10). This vector scores zero, as specified.

PASS 1 — REVISION

Three changes.

1. Direction softened from a finding to a failure-to-establish, with the tilt stated. I no longer say the median "is roughly +90 to +145 bp." I say: across every internally consistent screening convention I could apply, the median lands between +83 and +143 bp, and the only construction that clears 150 bp requires applying a use-of-proceeds screen to TransCanada PipeLines while not applying it to Bell and Enbridge — which is not a convention, it is a selection. The claim is unsupported on the retrievable record; the balance tilts against it.

2. Market-level triangulation added as independent support. The panel is six issuers; that is thin. But the same answer arrives from a completely different direction: the non-financial 2026 CAD maturity cohort carries a par-weighted coupon of 3.285% (RQO schedule, 2025-09-30) against 2026 CAD non-financial IG new-issue coupons averaging ≈4.31% — a gap of ≈+102 bp. And the whole CAD IG corporate market at the cutoff has average coupon 4.38% against YTM 4.32% (XCB, 2026-08-20), with the short end at coupon 4.09% vs YTM 3.80% (XSH, 2026-08-19). Two independent constructions, both around or below +100 bp, neither anywhere near +150 bp.

3. The TCPL row demoted and the conclusion made robust to its removal. Because TCPL is cbonds-only on the retired side and unmeasured on the issued side, I now present the panel both with and without it. Without TCPL the median is +128 bp; without TCPL and Enbridge, +117.5 bp. The conclusion does not depend on the row I am least able to defend. What TCPL contributes that survives its own weakness is the structural point — that the 2026 CAD maturity cohort contains legacy 1990s long bonds and 2023–24 peak-rate short paper, so the cohort's coupon is not uniformly low.

I also now state explicitly that my frame is a proxy that is known to undercount, so the panel is a sample of convenience, not the population the statistic names.

PASS 2 — SELF-ATTACK

PROVENANCE

1. My new market-level evidence is two documents from one source. XCB and XSH are both BlackRock funds and both figures came through a summarizing fetch. I cross-checked each against a second document type — XCB product page at 2026-08-20 (coupon 4.38%, YTM 4.32%) against the XCB fact-sheet PDF at 2026-07-31 (4.37%, 4.25%); XSH product page at 2026-08-19 (4.09%, 3.80%) against the XSH fact-sheet PDF at 2026-07-31 (4.08%, 3.79%). Coupons agree to 1 bp across document types and dates, so the extraction is sound. But BlackRock is one source. Under the rule that three outlets carrying one press release are one source, XCB-page and XCB-PDF are one source too.

2. My 3.285% cohort coupon is weighted by ETF par, not index face amount. The RQO par values cluster tightly (repeated ~14.8m and ~18.5m lines), which is the signature of a sampled and capped portfolio, not issue-size weighting. So "par-weighted" is close to equal-weighted and does not deliver the principal-weighting the statistic specifies. Recomputed equal-weighted across the twelve non-financial lines: 3.311% — 2.6 bp from the par-weighted figure. The defect is real but does not bite.

3. My 4.31% new-issue average had a population mismatch. It was a simple average across twelve deals, six of which belong to issuers with no identified 2026 maturity (407, NSPI, CT REIT, RioCan, Gibson, Waste Connections), and it ignored deal size. Recomputed principal-weighted across all twelve deals (C$9.70bn total): 4.386%. Gap versus the cohort coupon becomes +110 bp rather than +102 bp. The correction moves the number by 8 bp, not by 50.

4. A counter-source that looked fatal, and did not survive. Guardian Capital's Ahead of the Curve, published 2026-03-17, reports its GuardBonds™ 2026 Investment Grade Bond Fund (GBFC) with a current yield of 1.76% and duration 0.48 years at 2026-02-28 — implying an average coupon under 2% on residual 2026 IG paper, which would push every delta up sharply and support the claim. I retrieved the fund page. GBFC's top holdings at 2026-07-31 are 19.96% Province of Ontario, 9.80% CDP Financial Inc., 9.49% Bank of Nova Scotia, 9.48% AIMCo Realty, 9.48% Manulife Bank, 9.48% National Bank, 9.44% Desjardins, 9.23% Pembina, 7.02% Province of Quebec, 6.61% Bell Telephone Co. of Canada. Roughly a quarter provincials, most of the rest bank deposit notes of 2021 vintage. Its low current yield is a composition artifact, not a measurement of the non-financial corporate 2026 cohort. Attack repelled — but only because I went and looked.

5. Two exclusions upgraded from absence-of-evidence to positive evidence. AltaGas: its Q2 2026 results release of 2026-07-30 discloses long-term debt of $7,465m and subordinated hybrids of $2,205m with no 2026 issuance described. TELUS: its Q2 2026 MD&A records the 2026-05-08 partial redemption of the 2.75% Series CZ and no 2026 debt issuance in the section I could read.

6. Still unfixed after seven further attempts: TransCanada PipeLines' June 2026 coupons. DBRS by full slug, cbonds by ISIN, TC Energy's Q2 quarterly report with a targeted prompt, SEC EDGAR browse (robots-disallowed), MarketScreener, exchange listings — all failed. That cell of the panel remains estimated, not measured, and I will not pretend otherwise.

INFERENCE

Tenor mismatch inflates every positive delta in the panel. Bell retired paper with days to run and issued 7-, 10- and 30-year debt. Enbridge retired 2016-vintage paper and issued 5s, 10s and 30s. ARC retired a bond two weeks from maturity and issued 3s and 7s. 407 and TCPL issued 10s and 30s. The GoC curve at 2026-08-20 runs 3.35% at five years to 4.15% at thirty — 80 bp of pure term premium. A material share of every measured step-up is term extension, not the price of refinancing.

The claim as worded — "new coupon exceeds the retired coupon" — does count term extension, so my arithmetic is not wrong. But it means the statistic systematically overstates refinancing cost pressure, and any tenor-matched construction would come in lower. This attack pushes further below 150 bp rather than toward it; I record it because it is the strongest available argument that even my sub-threshold numbers are too high.

Ratings verification is thinner than my draft implied. "DBRS BBB- or higher at retirement" is a screen I asserted rather than checked for every row. What I can name: Finning BBB (high) (Morningstar DBRS confirmation); ARC's new notes BBB, Stable; Dream Industrial BBB (high), Stable; TCPL BBB (high), Stable (2026-06-24); Enbridge Inc. A (low), Stable (2026-06-26). Bell Canada was downgraded to BBB from BBB (high), with BCE to BBB (low), and its subordinated debt carries BBB (low) — all above the floor, but for Bell I am relying on DBRS rating-action titles surfaced in search results, not on documents I could open. Every row passes; two rows pass on weaker evidence than I claimed.

SELECTION

Two omissions an opponent would have found, both of which make my frame look worse:

  • Pembina Pipeline has a 2026 CAD maturity. GBFC holds Pembina at 9.23% of a fund defined as CAD IG bonds maturing in 2026 (holdings as at 2026-07-31). My research notes said only "Pembina — no debt offering in its 2026 news releases"; I had never established that it had a 2026 maturity. It does. Pembina still does not enter the panel — it issued nothing in the window — but I found this by accident, from a fund holdings table, not from my population frame.
  • North West Redwater has a second 2026 CAD maturity my frame missed: 3.2% due 2026-04-24 (cbonds, CA663307AE68), alongside the 2.000% due 2026-12-01 that RQO does list. The April bond was retired inside the window. NWR's last issuance was 2017, so it stays out of the panel — but again the frame missed it.

Both confirm the Pass 1 provenance finding and make it sharper: the RQO schedule is not a population, it is a convenience sample that I can demonstrate misses names, and I have no way to bound how many more it misses.

  • The claim's own origin is not retrievable. The prompt attributes the claim to "2026 maturity-wall thesis, sell-side and rating-agency credit commentary." I searched for that repeatedly. Guardian (2026-03-17), Canso (2026-01-20 and 2026-04-24), J. Zechner (2026-02-04 and 2026-05-05), Renaissance (January 2026), Breckinridge (2026-07-07), S&P Global Ratings' refinancing credit-trends series, and the Bank of Canada's FSR (2026-05-28) all discuss 2026 issuance, spreads, valuations and refinancing generally. None of them states a Canadian coupon-refresh figure, and none states anything like 150 bp. I could not locate the source the claim came from. That is a finding in its own right: the threshold is not traceable to any published document in the reachable record, so I cannot check my panel against the construction whoever wrote it had in mind.

ANACHRONISM

Zero. Latest sources: Bank of Canada yields 2026-08-20; XCB 2026-08-20; XSH 2026-08-19; Finning pricing 2026-08-10; AltaGas Q2 2026-07-30; XSH/XCB fact sheets 2026-07-31; GBFC holdings 2026-07-31.

PASS 2 — REVISION

1. Market-level triangulation restated with corrected weights. The like-for-like comparison is now: non-financial 2026 CAD maturity cohort coupon 3.285% par-weighted / 3.311% equal-weighted (RQO schedule, 2025-09-30) against 2026 CAD non-financial IG new issuance 4.386% principal-weighted across C$9.70bn of deals (twelve issuer primary releases). Gap ≈ +110 bp. This now sits almost exactly on the six-issuer panel median of +110 bp, from an independent construction.

2. The GBFC counter-source is neutralized in the record, not ignored. It is the one piece of retrievable evidence that on its face supports a large step-up, and the reason it fails — a fund half-composed of provincials and bank deposit notes — is stated rather than assumed.

3. A new reason the measured deltas are upper bounds. Because Canadian issuers in 2026 systematically refinanced short paper with 7-to-30-year paper, roughly up to 80 bp of every positive delta in the panel is GoC term premium rather than refinancing cost. The claim's wording counts it, so I keep it in the numbers — but the "refinancing got 150 bp more expensive" reading of the claim is weaker still than the arithmetic reading, and the arithmetic reading already fails.

4. The threshold is unsourced. I now state plainly that I could not find, anywhere in the retrievable dated record, the sell-side or rating-agency publication that produces a 150 bp Canadian coupon-step-up figure. I am testing a number whose provenance I cannot establish.

5. Frame incompleteness restated more severely. Pass 1 said the frame "undercounts." Pass 2 shows it missed a maturity at Pembina and a second maturity at North West Redwater that I found only by accident. The panel is a sample of convenience whose coverage of the true population I cannot bound. That is the single largest limitation on the whole exercise, and it is now stated as such rather than as a caveat.

PASS 3 — SELF-ATTACK

PROVENANCE

1. I finally found the ground rules for my own frame, and they change what it means. FTSE Canada Maturity Corporate Bond Index Series — Ground Rules, July 2026 (v5.0) (lseg.com). Eligibility: minimum issue size $250mm; BBB or higher; CAD; corporations incorporated under Canadian federal/provincial law. Excluded: floating-rate, zero-coupon, convertible/amortizing, asset-backed, PPP, index-linked, bank/insurer Tier 1 — and "trust-issued bonds (including REITs)" and "callable securities." Construction is a yield-ranked selection with a 10% issuer cap and a 25% BBB cap, not full replication.

This explains every gap I complained about in Passes 1 and 2, and it means my Pass-2 framing ("a convenience sample whose coverage I cannot bound") was wrong in a specific way — the exclusions are systematic and now known. Finning (~$180mm) fails the size test. Bell M-41, TELUS Series CV and Capital Power's 4.986% are callable MTNs and are excluded by rule. Dream Industrial, RioCan and CT REIT are trust-issued and excluded by rule. But it also means the frame is biased: it selects large, non-callable, bullet corporate bonds, and it drops exactly the callable MTN population that skews to more recent, higher-coupon vintages. My earlier statement that the frame merely "undercounts" was too kind; it undercounts non-randomly, in a direction that makes the retired side look lower-coupon than it is — i.e. in the direction that favours the claim. My panel is therefore, if anything, tilted toward the claim, and it still fails.

2. A REIT-exclusion scare that does not apply. The Maturity index series excludes REITs, which would knock Dream Industrial (+18 bp) out of my panel and lift the median. But the claim's stated population is the S&P Canada IG Corporate Bond Index or the FTSE Canada Universe Bond Index corporate sub-index, and the FTSE Universe corporate sub-index carries an explicit Real Estate sector weight in its own factsheet. REIT debentures are in the named population. Dream Industrial stays.

3. My two historical index anchors are stale template documents and must be labelled as history, not as current facts. The LSEG FTSE Canada factsheet — All Corporate on lseg.com carries data as at 30 December 2022; the FTSE Canada Universe Bond Index factsheet carries data as at 31 July 2023. Both are dated well before the cutoff, so they are admissible — but only as statements about 2022 and 2023, and I must not let them stand in for 2026.

4. The market-level evidence now has genuine source independence, which it did not in Pass 2. BMO Global Asset Management, a different manager with a different data pipeline from BlackRock, publishes the same two FTSE indices:

  • ZCB, FTSE Canada All Corporate Bond Index, as at 2026-07-31: coupon 4.34%, YTM 4.21%. BlackRock's XCB at the same date: 4.37% / 4.25%. Agreement within 4 bp.
  • ZCS, FTSE Canada Short Term Corporate Bond Index, as at 2026-07-31: coupon 4.15%, YTM 3.77%. BlackRock's XSH at the same date: 4.08% / 3.79%. Agreement within 7 bp.

Two managers, four documents, two index families. This clears the Pass-2 objection that XCB and XCB-PDF were one source.

5. Still unmeasured, and I will stop pretending otherwise: TransCanada PipeLines' June 2026 CAD coupons, after roughly ten distinct retrieval attempts. And I never verified the principal of Enbridge Gas's 2.50% note due 2026-08-05 — my draft carried "~$300m", which was an assumption dressed as a figure. That is the one place in this document where I wrote a number I did not have. It is corrected below.

INFERENCE

The index-level evidence is not the claim's statistic, and I must not let it pretend to be. Coupon-versus-YTM on the whole corporate stock measures the refinancing gap on all outstanding paper, dominated by 2023–25 vintages. The claim is about the 2026-maturity cohort specifically, which is a below-average-coupon subset (3.285–3.311% versus an index-wide 4.34%). So the index gap of roughly zero does not directly imply the cohort gap is zero — the cohort gap is positive. The index evidence bounds and corroborates; it does not substitute. My Pass-2 revision leaned on it slightly too hard.

Stated as a falsification condition, which is the honest form. For the claim to hold, one of two things must be true: the retired-side 2026 non-financial cohort must carry an average coupon of about 2.9% or lower against ~4.4% new issuance; or 2026 CAD non-financial IG new issuance must average about 4.8%+ against a ~3.3% cohort. Neither is what the record shows. Measured cohort coupon: 3.285% par-weighted, 3.311% equal-weighted. Measured new issuance: 4.386% principal-weighted across C$9.70bn of twelve deals. The gap is +110 bp, and it would need to be 40 bp wider on one side or the other.

The break-even on my thin panel. Six observations (−75, +18, +92, +128, +143, +161; median +110) invites the objection that one or two missing issuers flip it. They do not. Adding two unobserved issuers with deltas above +150 gives a median of +135.5; adding three gives +143; it takes four or more additional issuers, every one of them above +150 bp and none below, to lift the median past the threshold. Given that I have checked roughly thirty-five Canadian non-financial IG names and that every low-coupon 2026 maturity I found (Saputo 1.415%, BCI QuadReal 1.073%, North West Redwater 2.000% and 3.2%, Pembina, AltaGas 4.638%, Hydro One 2.770%, TELUS, Enbridge Gas/Pipelines) is excluded for a documented reason rather than for want of looking, four unobserved qualifying high-delta issuers is a strong assumption.

SELECTION

The strongest evidence in this whole exercise, which I did not have until now, and the strongest pro-claim number, which is three years stale. Both come from the index publisher and its licensed trackers:

| Index | As at | Avg coupon | YTM | Coupon-to-yield gap |

|---|---|---|---|---|

| FTSE Canada All Corporate (LSEG factsheet) | 2022-12-30 | 3.70% | 5.27% | +157 bp |

| FTSE Canada Universe corporate sub-index (LSEG factsheet) | 2023-07-31 | 3.90% | 5.49% | +159 bp |

| FTSE Canada All Corporate (BMO ZCB) | 2026-07-31 | 4.34% | 4.21% | −13 bp |

| FTSE Canada All Corporate (iShares XCB) | 2026-07-31 | 4.37% | 4.25% | −12 bp |

| FTSE Canada All Corporate (iShares XCB) | 2026-08-20 | 4.38% | 4.32% | −6 bp |

| FTSE Canada Short Term Corporate (BMO ZCS) | 2026-07-31 | 4.15% | 3.77% | −38 bp |

| FTSE Canada Universe + Maple Short Term Corporate (iShares XSH) | 2026-08-19 | 4.09% | 3.80% | −29 bp |

This is the claim's own thesis, dated. In the named population — the FTSE Canada Universe corporate sub-index — the market-wide gap between what issuers were paying and what they would pay to refinance was +159 bp at 31 July 2023, essentially the claim's threshold. By July–August 2026 the same gap across the corporate index is negative, and negative at the short end too. Four years of high-coupon issuance reset the coupon stock upward while yields came down; the maturity-wall arithmetic that was correct in 2022–23 has been spent.

An opponent arguing for the claim would cite the 2023 row. It is the best number available to them and it is three years before the period the claim is about.

ANACHRONISM

Zero. Nothing cited postdates 2026-08-22; the historical index rows are explicitly labelled 2022 and 2023.

PASS 3 — REVISION

1. The number I did not have is removed. The Enbridge row no longer assumes a size for the 2.50% note due 2026-08-05. Using only the two verified repayments (2.81% × C$250m in June 2026; 3.00% × C$400m in May 2026), Enbridge's principal-weighted retired coupon is 2.927% and its delta is +120 bp. Admitting the August note at any principal between C$100m and C$500m moves the delta only to +126 to +139 bp. So the Enbridge row is +120 to +139 bp, bounded, with no invented figure. Panel median is unaffected at every point in that range.

2. The evidential hierarchy is inverted. The load-bearing evidence is now the index-level arithmetic, with the six-issuer panel as corroboration rather than as the finding:

  • Cohort versus new issuance: non-financial 2026 CAD maturity cohort 3.285%/3.311% against 2026 CAD non-financial IG new issuance 4.386% principal-weighted → +110 bp.
  • Whole-market context: FTSE Canada All Corporate coupon 4.38% vs YTM 4.32% at 2026-08-20 (XCB), corroborated at 4.34%/4.21% by BMO ZCB at 2026-07-31; short-term corporate coupon 4.09% vs YTM 3.80% (XSH, 2026-08-19), corroborated at 4.15%/3.77% by ZCS. The aggregate Canadian IG refinancing gap at the cutoff is zero to slightly negative.
  • Historical arc: the same gap was +157 bp (Dec 2022) and +159 bp (Jul 2023, Universe corporate sub-index). The claim is a 2022–23 fact carried into 2026.
  • Panel: six issuers, median +110 bp; +128 bp without TransCanada PipeLines; +143 bp in the most claim-favourable internally consistent variant.

3. The frame's bias is now stated with its direction. The FTSE Maturity Corporate ground rules (July 2026) exclude callable MTNs, REIT/trust issues and sub-$250mm issues. Callable MTNs skew to more recent, higher-coupon vintages, so excluding them makes the retired cohort look lower-coupon than the true population — biasing my measured step-up upward, toward the claim. The claim fails anyway.

4. Break-even stated instead of implied. Four or more unobserved qualifying issuers, all above +150 bp, would be required to lift the panel median past the threshold. Three would not.

5. What remains genuinely unresolved is named rather than buried: TransCanada PipeLines' June 2026 coupons were never retrieved and that row's magnitude is an estimate (its sign is robust — its blended new coupon would have to exceed 5.83%, i.e. roughly 165–195 bp over the June 2026 GoC curve, at a moment when Morningstar DBRS rated it BBB (high) and long BBB spreads were ~140 bp); and no publication in the reachable dated record states the 150 bp Canadian figure the claim is built on.

FINAL CONCLUSION

Direction: the claim is not supported. Every construction of the statistic I could build from the retrievable dated record puts the issuer-level median coupon step-up between roughly +83 and +143 basis points — below the 150 bp the claim asserts — and the market-level analogue lands at about +110 bp. I do not claim the statistic has been measured; I claim it has been bounded, repeatedly, on the wrong side of the threshold for the claim.

The evidence

1. Like-for-like cohort arithmetic (+110 bp). The par-weighted average coupon of the non-financial CAD 2026-maturity cohort is 3.285% (equal-weighted 3.311%), computed line by line from the RBC Target 2026 Canadian Corporate Bond Index ETF's schedule of investment portfolio as at 2025-09-30. Against it, twelve CAD non-financial IG new issues settled between 2026-01-01 and 2026-08-22, totalling C$9.70bn, average 4.386% principal-weighted — every coupon taken from an issuer press release, prospectus supplement or quarterly financial statement. Gap ≈ +110 bp.

2. A six-issuer panel built to the claim's own screens (median +110 bp). Issuers that were non-financial, CAD, DBRS BBB- or better, retired a 2026-maturing fixed-rate CAD bond on or before 2026-08-22, and settled new CAD fixed-rate issuance inside the window:

| Issuer | Weighted retired coupon | Weighted new CAD coupon | Δ |

|---|---|---|---|

| TransCanada PipeLines | 5.830% (8.29%×240.6m; 4.35%×400m) | est. 4.8–5.5% — not retrieved | negative, ≈ −75 bp |

| Dream Industrial REIT | 3.968% (×200m) | 4.150% (×200m) | +18 bp |

| Bell Canada | 3.864% (8.875%×125m; 2.90%×650m) | 4.783% (4.40%×750m; 4.70%×900m; 5.30%×700m) | +92 bp |

| Enbridge (consolidated) | 2.927% verified (2.81%×250m; 3.00%×400m) | 4.131% (3.57%×850m; 4.35%×850m; 5.10%×300m) | +120 to +139 bp |

| ARC Resources | 2.354% (×450m) | 3.786% (3.349%×400m; 4.104%×550m) | +143 bp |

| Finning International | 2.626% (×~180m) | 4.231% (×300m) | +161 bp |

Median +110 bp. Without TransCanada PipeLines (the weakest-sourced row): +128 bp. Under the most claim-favourable internally consistent variant — excluding Enbridge for legal-issuer mismatch and treating Bell's subordinated debenture as out of scope: +143 bp. Reading the claim as median(new) − median(retired) rather than median(new − retired): +83 bp.

3. Market-level corroboration, from two managers on the same indices. FTSE Canada All Corporate Bond Index: average coupon 4.38% against YTM 4.32% at 2026-08-20 (iShares XCB), corroborated at 4.34% / 4.21% at 2026-07-31 by BMO ZCB. FTSE Canada Short Term Corporate: coupon 4.09% vs YTM 3.80% at 2026-08-19 (iShares XSH), corroborated at 4.15% / 3.77% by BMO ZCS. The aggregate Canadian IG corporate refinancing gap at the cutoff is zero to slightly negative.

4. The claim is a 2022–23 fact carried into 2026. In the claim's own named population — the FTSE Canada Universe Bond Index corporate sub-index — the coupon-to-yield gap was +159 bp at 2023-07-31 (average coupon 3.90%, YTM 5.49%, LSEG factsheet), and +157 bp across FTSE Canada All Corporate at 2022-12-30 (3.70% / 5.27%). That is the 150 bp figure, and it was true then. Four years of high-coupon issuance reset the coupon stock upward while yields fell; by 2026 the same gap is negative. The maturity-wall arithmetic has been spent.

5. Why the mechanism did not produce the number. The 5-year Canada yield did rise from 0.54% in 2021 to 3.10% in 2026 (Canso, 2026-04-24). But credit spreads offset much of it — "multi-decade tight" (J. Zechner, 2026-02-04), long BBB at 140 bp and 72 bp inside its long-run average (Canso, 2026-01-20), IG spreads "in the lowest percentile" in Canada and the US (Bank of Canada FSR, 2026-05-28). And the 2026 maturity cohort is not uniformly low-coupon: it contains 1990s long bonds (TransCanada PipeLines 8.29%, Bell Canada 8.875%) and 2023–24 peak-rate short paper (Rogers 5.65%, Suncor 5.40%, Capital Power 4.986%, which refinanced 75 bp cheaper).

6. Robustness. It would take four or more unobserved qualifying issuers, every one above +150 bp and none below, to lift the panel median past the threshold; three would not. I checked roughly thirty-five Canadian non-financial IG names, and every low-coupon 2026 maturity I found (Saputo 1.415%, BCI QuadReal 1.073%, North West Redwater 2.000% and 3.2%, Pembina, Hydro One 2.770%, TELUS 2.75%/3.75%, AltaGas 4.638%, Enbridge Gas/Pipelines) is out for a documented reason — no in-window CAD issuance, or a maturity falling after the cutoff — not for want of looking. The frame's own bias runs toward the claim: the FTSE Maturity Corporate ground rules (July 2026) exclude callable MTNs, which skew to higher-coupon recent vintages, making the retired side look lower-coupon than it is.

Where the evidence stops

  • One panel cell was never measured. TransCanada PipeLines' June 2026 CAD tranches — C$700m due 2036 and C$600m due 2056, issued 2026-06-25 — have no retrievable coupons after roughly ten attempts. The row's sign is robust (its blended coupon would have to exceed 5.83%, i.e. ~165–195 bp over the June 2026 GoC curve, when Morningstar DBRS rated it BBB (high) and long BBB spreads were ~140 bp), but its magnitude is an estimate.
  • The panel is not the population, and no public source gives me the population. My retired-side frame is a rule-based selection — minimum $250mm, non-callable, no trust/REIT issues, yield-ranked with issuer and rating caps — not a census. I found qualifying maturities (Pembina, North West Redwater's April note) only by accident, from a competitor fund's holdings table. Six observations is a thin median.
  • Two panel rows turn on definitional choices the claim does not settle: whether Enbridge Inc., Enbridge Gas Inc. and Enbridge Pipelines Inc. are one issuer or three (they are one group; only the parent issued, only the subsidiaries retired), and whether Bell Canada's 8.875% subordinated debenture counts as retired 2026 fixed-rate debt. Each moves the median by 15–35 bp.
  • "Replacement" is undefined. Only two of six issuers — ARC Resources and Finning — stated a use-of-proceeds link to the 2026 maturity. Bell's June proceeds went to tender offers for 2028–2047 paper; Enbridge's February MTNs were general corporate. A use-of-proceeds screen applied consistently would leave a three-issuer panel with a median of +143 bp; applied inconsistently it can be made to yield +161 bp, which is a selection, not a convention.
  • Term extension inflates every positive delta. Issuers refinanced near-dated paper with 7-to-30-year debt across an 80 bp GoC term premium. The claim's wording counts it; a refinancing-cost reading would come in lower still.
  • The 150 bp threshold is not traceable. I could not find, in any dated retrievable publication — Canso, J. Zechner, Guardian, Renaissance, Breckinridge, S&P Global Ratings, Morningstar DBRS, the Bank of Canada — a Canadian coupon-step-up figure at all, let alone 150 bp. I am testing a number whose published source I cannot locate.

NARROWED CLAIMS

1. Direction, narrowed from a finding to a failure-to-establish. Drafted: "the claim is not supported… the issuer-level median coupon step-up is roughly +90 to +145 basis points." Narrowed to: "On every internally consistent construction I could build from retrievable sources, the median step-up falls between +83 and +143 bp; the claim is unsupported on the retrievable record and the balance of evidence tilts against it. The statistic has been bounded, not measured, and the claim is not shown to be false."

2. The Enbridge row, narrowed from a point estimate built on an assumed size to a verified bound. Drafted: "≈ +128 bp", using an unverified "~$300m" for the Enbridge Gas 2.50% note due 2026-08-05. Narrowed to: "+120 bp on the two verified repayments alone (2.81% × C$250m; 3.00% × C$400m); +120 to +139 bp across any plausible principal for the August note." The invented figure is withdrawn.

3. The TransCanada PipeLines row, narrowed from a measured delta to a signed but unmeasured one. Drafted: "≈ −75 bp." Narrowed to: "negative in sign — its blended new coupon would need to exceed 5.83%, roughly 165–195 bp over the June 2026 GoC curve, against a BBB (high) rating and ~140 bp long BBB spreads — but the magnitude is an estimate, not a measurement, because the coupons were never retrieved."

4. The population frame, narrowed from "the index" to a described selection. Drafted as "the best retrievable primary enumeration." Narrowed to: "a rule-based selection under the FTSE Canada Maturity Corporate Bond Index Series ground rules (July 2026) — minimum $250mm, BBB or higher, excluding callable securities, trust/REIT issues, floating-rate and several other classes, yield-ranked with a 10% issuer cap and 25% BBB cap — which is not the population the claim names and is biased, in a direction that favours the claim."

5. The market-level evidence, narrowed against over-reach. The near-zero index-wide coupon-to-yield gap does not imply the cohort gap is zero; the 2026 cohort is a below-average-coupon subset and its gap is genuinely positive at about +110 bp. The index data bounds and corroborates; it does not substitute for the cohort statistic.

CONCESSION

CONCESSION: MOVED — the direction survived all three passes, but the self-attack forced four substantive changes: I withdrew an invented principal amount in the Enbridge row, downgraded the TransCanada row from measured to signed-only, inverted the evidential hierarchy so index-level arithmetic rather than a six-issuer panel carries the conclusion, and restated the finding as a bounded failure-to-establish rather than a measured range — while Pass 3 added the decisive item the draft entirely lacked, the FTSE Canada Universe corporate sub-index coupon-to-yield gap of +159 bp at 2023-07-31 collapsing to −6 bp by 2026-08-20, which reframes the claim as a stale 2022–23 fact rather than a false one.
